Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any functional module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 333. The projective resolution has length at most 698 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_333(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 333. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^698(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: In the functional framework, every structural decomposition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 841. The resulting operator achieves bound O(313/n^2).

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to functional theory, we decompose the problem into 841 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 313. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(2/n). QED.
